Plan seven independent rounds; **184 open / 17 held / 68 hard / 99 cheap** on Lamp main `c77030f1bf9501975efca38be82a33d9611a9d5a`.

The status table has 60 done,129 partial and55 missing clauses. These counts retain its judgments: CHEAP includes already-built clauses needing independent proof/recheck, not99 missing features. HARD describes the whole clause even when a useful bounded slice fits one round. HELD-EXTERNAL names the actual absent authority/interface; separate field receipts do not stop otherwise buildable local work. No builder may edit Status, Gap or Evidence. ACCEPTANCE.md is current and docs/CLAIMS.md reports24 claims:1 done,23 partial. Only1 claim is currently judged done; this audit does not promote any.

Inspected the repository source, acceptance requirements and tests,873 cloud refs and recent main. No product source was authored or changed; the disposable read-only clone was advanced to the newly observed published main for a final delta check and is clean. The evidence publisher must preserve that terminal HEAD/index/worktree. The generated-document check passed (0 errors/warnings); four syntax checks passed. Browser/behavior suites were inspected, not run: no dependencies, build, server, deployment or paid API was used. The source audit began at `4c90e6c8f35775e2a0287ab24ccba87d7a8d8485`; terminal main advanced by the single health-ledger commit to the SHA above, with contracts/feature implementations unchanged. Actual commands and limitations are in planning-receipt.json. The read-only audits used the requested gpt-6.1-sol/high model. No product commit/push, PR, CI dispatch or launch is authorized or performed.

The buyer priorities are trustworthy public incident content, safe restore custody, actionable delivery records, self-service webhook compatibility, reliable phone operation, useful maintenance calendars and pre-incident sending-rate advice. The seven queued scopes below exclude already-built mechanisms and preserve existing gates. Their implementation/test scopes are independent; contract status, release/migration inventory consolidation and final integration remain feeder/lander work. Any new migrations need unique names, existing migrations remain immutable, and source-lock/registration conflicts must be reconciled without weakening another round.

Existing sd work is reserved even where its activity cannot be inferred from branch existence: THEME-004/005 fixes and all-nine-colour/card journeys in `cloud/sd-lamp-c-theme45` (`d325ad0d2008` ancestry); NOTIFY-003 exact-hour retry in `cloud/sd-lamp-c-f74`/`-c1` (`72edf10404`/`6ccae9d9734f`); durable UPDATE-007 reviewer provenance in `cloud/sd-lamp-c-f73`; attribution f127, impact f126, Reach f118, Statuspage readers f119, imports/templates f121, capacity f128/f132, public catalogue f12 and the other proof families named below. These are not new queue entries. A stale status is neither a fresh build request nor a license to land an old branch unchanged.

The just-landed health-ledger round is `cloud/sd-lamp-health-ledger-1-c1-rebase-c77030f1bf9501975efca38be82a33d9611a9d5a` at `c77030f1bf9501975efca38be82a33d9611a9d5a`: its changes are already main and its import/setup/platform-ledger paths and tests remain outside this queue. R2 two-generation retention (`e569004d`), resumable deploy choices (`7c17a320`), native mail-off and1.11.22 metadata are already main; no duplicate rollout/release work is queued. Status.io saved-history and incident.io public-history readers also have existing partial branches; adopting them does not fulfill the full keyed/multichannel migration clauses.

Material source findings: custom CSS/HTML and vendor catalog/show/draft/rollup already ship; DOCS-001’s index and PAGE-008’s zero-DB/cache gaps are now explicitly tested; internal components, exact directory pagination, prelaunch, signed unsubscribe encodings, complete1000-recipient acceptance and actual Queue replay have newer proof. Conversely, current main really loses palette choices, has fixed-light card defects, loses reviewer provenance after publication and uses a61-minute retry plateau; existing sd branches own those repairs. PORT-001’s retained chat ciphertext remains an unclaimed restore-safety gap. UPDATE-005 also has a contract/source tension: main intentionally carries affected-audience wording into the next draft, despite the old empty-next-draft wording. Keep that visible for owner/judge resolution rather than silently changing product behavior or metadata.

#### DOCS-001 — Public owner documentation

Status: partial; planning class: CHEAP; CONTRACT line 3193.

**Requirement.** Public owner guidance is available on the app host, including the selected published Markdown pages; it is separate from the status-page host.

**Acceptance.**
1. A visitor without an operator session can read the documentation index and each selected published page on the app host.
2. A selected page renders its repository Markdown, and documentation is absent from the public status-page host.

Current-main planning reconciliation: Built: unauthenticated/no-JS index navigation and every selected Markdown page are asserted; no new implementation.

#### PAGE-001 — Contents

Status: partial; planning class: CHEAP; CONTRACT line 3194.

**Requirement.** One initial public response contains the complete status overview, current incident information and relevant maintenance, with component uptime.

**Acceptance.**
1. The response shows overall status and every visible component, with each current state and note.
2. Every unresolved incident shows its five most recent published updates, newest first, and a link to its complete permanent timeline.
3. Every maintenance in progress and each scheduled maintenance starting within 14 days appears.
4. Each visible component has its uptime figure and the daily record for the reporting window.

Recorded Evidence: `tests/e2e/public-contract.test.mjs`

Recorded Gap: Old cited evidence asserts Overall status appears; The total daily-mark count is between six times ninety and six times ninety-one. Unasserted: Every visible component is present; Each visible component’s current state is present; Each visible component’s exact note is present; Every unresolved incident appears; Five most recent updates are shown in newest-first order; Each incident links its complete permanent timeline; All in-progress maintenance appears; The 14-day scheduled-maintenance inclusion boundary is asserted; Daily-mark coverage is reconciled separately for every visible component; Each visible component uptime figure is asserted.

Current-main planning reconciliation: Built renderer; prove every unresolved incident’s exact latest five updates/newest-first/permalink, all in-progress maintenance and exact 14-day scheduled boundary. Reconcile component state/note/uptime separately with existing f12 work.

#### PAGE-005 — Snapshot read path

Status: partial; planning class: CHEAP; CONTRACT line 3198.

**Requirement.** Public page, Atom feed and widget content are published as a complete retained snapshot, and remain available while publication recovers.

**Acceptance.**
1. Each change to PAGE-001 content produces a complete rendered public page, Atom feed and widget snapshot.
2. Serving those three surfaces reads the snapshot without any database query.
3. A failed snapshot write is retried until success; the prior snapshot continues serving during recovery.

Recorded Evidence: `tests/e2e/publishing-contract.test.mjs`, `tests/e2e/snapshot-retained.test.mjs`

Recorded Gap: Old cited evidence asserts A rendered public-page artifact exists; An Atom-feed artifact exists; A widget artifact exists; A failed build remains stale until a retry. Unasserted: Every PAGE-001 change triggers a complete snapshot; The public page remains readable without the database; The feed remains readable without the database; The widget remains readable without the database; The old publication body is read back while the unrecoverable replica failure is in force; Public snapshot reads issue no database query rather than merely surviving missing tables.

Current-main planning reconciliation: Zero-DB reads, complete artifacts and failed-replica old-body recovery are tested; remaining complete PAGE-001 change-trigger matrix.

#### PAGE-006 — Serving through a failure

Status: partial; planning class: CHEAP; CONTRACT line 3199.

**Requirement.** Public status continues to answer from retained content during primary storage, database or queue failure, with truthful stale labeling.

**Acceptance.**
1. When the database, queue or object storage is unreachable, the page, Atom feed and widget return successful current or retained content; an origin outage can use PAGE-007’s cached response.
2. Only an installation with no snapshot ever written returns unavailable instead of status content.
3. Content not built from current data identifies its generation instant and says Last known state; a failed read never invents an operational all-clear.

Recorded Evidence: `tests/e2e/public-contract.test.mjs`, `tests/e2e/public-contract.test.mjs`, `tests/e2e/publishing-contract.test.mjs`

Recorded Gap: Old cited evidence asserts The page body still contains Partially unavailable after database removal; Stale page content is labeled Last known state; The generation instant is visible in stale page content; The retained failure body contains Partially unavailable. Unasserted: The Atom feed survives database outage; The widget survives database outage; Page content survives queue outage; Atom feed content survives queue outage; Widget content survives queue outage; Page content survives object-storage outage; Atom feed content survives object-storage outage; Widget content survives object-storage outage; Origin outage uses the specified cached-response window; Page is unavailable only before first publication; Atom feed is unavailable only before first publication; Widget is unavailable only before first publication.

Current-main planning reconciliation: DB/queue/R2/cold-restart retained page/feed/widget are tested; prove pre-first-publication Atom/widget unavailability and exact one-hour origin-cache boundary.

#### PAGE-007 — Publish latency and caching

Status: partial; planning class: CHEAP; CONTRACT line 3200.

**Requirement.** Publications become visible promptly through every public representation, and healthy reads validate the current generation.

**Acceptance.**
1. Stable public addresses validate the current generation when reused; unchanged content can be reused, while a newer generation makes older cached bytes ineligible for a healthy response.
2. An update, component-state change or maintenance transition reaches the page, history, incident and maintenance permanent pages, Atom feed, widget and public JSON within 10 seconds of the initiating successful request.
3. An unreachable origin can use retained content for one hour; retained last-known snapshots follow PAGE-006.

Recorded Evidence: `tests/e2e/public-contract.test.mjs`, `tests/e2e/public-contract.test.mjs`, `tests/e2e/snapshot-retained.test.mjs`

Recorded Gap: The cited evidence asserts the other audited outcomes; unasserted: Advancing a generation makes all earlier healthy cache entries ineligible; Update publication reaches public page within ten seconds; Update publication reaches history within ten seconds; Update publication reaches incident permanent page within ten seconds; Update publication reaches maintenance permanent page within ten seconds; Update publication reaches Atom feed within ten seconds; Update publication reaches widget within ten seconds; Update publication reaches public JSON within ten seconds; Component-state change reaches public page within ten seconds; Component-state change reaches history within ten seconds; Component-state change reaches incident permanent page within ten seconds; Component-state change reaches maintenance permanent page within ten seconds; Component-state change reaches Atom feed within ten seconds; Component-state change reaches widget within ten seconds; Component-state change reaches public JSON within ten seconds; Maintenance transition reaches public page within ten seconds; Maintenance transition reaches history within ten seconds; Maintenance transition reaches incident permanent page within ten seconds; Maintenance transition reaches maintenance permanent page within ten seconds; Maintenance transition reaches Atom feed within ten seconds; Maintenance transition reaches widget within ten seconds; Maintenance transition reaches public JSON within ten seconds; Origin-failure retention lasts exactly the promised one-hour window. Additional freshness assertions are in tests/e2e/helpers/public-freshness-flow.mjs, which the old Evidence cell did not cite.

Current-main planning reconciliation: Healthy generation invalidates warmed old ETags; prove update/component/maintenance trigger × page/history/incident/maintenance/Atom/widget/JSON ≤10s, plus exact 1h stale expiry. Cited public-freshness-flow helper is absent.

#### PAGE-008 — Public read paths are not rate limited

Status: partial; planning class: CHEAP; CONTRACT line 3201.

**Requirement.** Public status reads are exempt from per-caller rate limiting so shared customer egress stays usable during an incident. Supersedes `BASE-ACCESS-003` for those paths only. Public writes continue under that duty.

**Acceptance.**
1. All public reads up to the declared per-address capacity succeed and never return a rate-limit refusal.
2. Public reads share immutable generation content; healthy reads validate the current generation without querying the database, with artifact caching reducing object-byte reads.

Recorded Evidence: `tests/e2e/public-contract.test.mjs`; `tests/e2e/snapshot-retained.test.mjs`; six fresh generation artifacts each require one initial blob-byte read, three repeated warm rounds and conditional reads preserve identical bytes and generation identity without more byte reads, and every warm read still validates the current pointer. A subsequent generation displaces the old validators and reads its own artifacts. The cache assertions are a proposal pending clause judgment; main's partial status and recorded gap are retained.

Recorded Gap: The cited evidence asserts the other audited outcomes; unasserted: Healthy generation validation performs no database query; Artifact caching reduces object-byte reads.

Current-main planning reconciliation: Both recorded gaps already asserted: zero DB touches and warm immutable-byte-read reduction; no new implementation.

#### PAGE-023 — Independent DNS failover

Status: missing; planning class: HARD; CONTRACT line 3210.

*Tier:* Core · *From:* PAGE-015

**Requirement.** The owner can configure independently hosted DNS failover and verify it end to end without Lamp operating DNS. The exact health-check configuration is documented in the public mechanisms record. The formats and configuration details are in [Public status mechanisms](docs/contract/public-details.md).

**Acceptance.**
1. Settings provides Amazon Route 53 failover routing and IBM NS1 Connect up/down-monitor and priority-filter settings.
2. The primary serving probe succeeds only when it can serve the current object-storage generation, stays healthy during a database outage and fails when object storage is unavailable.
3. Setup checks primary resolution, the exact secondary record, secondary TLS for the buyer hostname and newest generation, and the serving-probe target; every failed step is named, including a missing secondary record.
4. With ten-second checks, failure threshold three and sixty-second TTL, the buyer hostname serves the labeled newest secondary generation within 180 seconds of primary failure; overload beyond the provider response window also fails over.
5. A local DNS harness uses the same check interval, threshold and TTL; a hosted run uses a configured Route 53 test zone.
6. Failback occurs when the primary probe recovers; ongoing mirror writes ensure it serves content no older than failover did.

Recorded Evidence: `src/core/app/host-dispatch.ts`, `src/core/features/page-settings/service.ts`, `tests/e2e/public-contract.test.mjs`

Recorded Gap: The serving probe, failover configuration, local DNS harness, setup check and failover/failback journey are absent.

Current-main planning reconciliation: All six points remain: Route53/NS1 settings, storage-only serving probe, exact resolution/TLS/secondary checks, ≤180s failure/overload switch, matching local DNS harness/hosted zone, fresh failback.

4. A standby journey reads the summary API, RSS feed and badge from one generation and observes an unavailable subscription attempt with retry guidance.

#### SUB-005 — Cookieless subscriber tokens

Status: partial; planning class: CHEAP; CONTRACT line 3300.

Valid management changes only its own subscription. Native deletion of pending and active controls revokes every issued scope; old confirmation, management and unsubscribe authority cannot restore or change any subscription, while an unrelated management link remains usable. The same journey and `tests/e2e/helpers/subscriber-preferences-flow.mjs` (subscriberPreferencesFlow) prove management refusal and unchanged records after unsubscribe. Token responses retain referrer suppression, indexing suppression and no cookies. Additional candidate assertions supplement the evidence; main's partial judgment and recorded gap remain authoritative.

Recorded Gap: Old cited evidence asserts referrer suppression; indexing suppression. Unasserted: one-subscription scope and no unrelated capability; at least 128 bits of entropy; a previously usable management token is refused after unsubscribe; deletion revokes every token.

Current-main planning reconciliation: Token scope/HMAC authority/substitution/revocation proof now present; existing f92 owns recheck, no login redesign.

#### SUB-013 — SMS subscribers

Status: partial; planning class: HELD-EXTERNAL; CONTRACT line 3308.

*Tier:* Core · *From:* SUB-013 · *Cites:* BASE-MAIL-001

**Requirement.** Visitors and operators add consenting mobile subscribers. US and Canadian numbers are accepted by default; the owner chooses any additional countries. SUB-021 governs SMS link rendering.

**Acceptance.**
1. A visitor submits a valid international-format number; malformed or disabled-country numbers are refused.
2. One opt-in text per number per 24 hours names the page, frequency, rates notice and HELP/STOP keywords.
3. Only a YES reply activates the subscription, retaining number, consent time, page and exact wording as TCPA evidence.
4. STOP, UNSUBSCRIBE, CANCEL, END and QUIT each deactivate immediately and send the single carrier-required confirmation; HELP returns the page and support contact.
5. A Twilio test-account journey subscribes a US number, confirms with YES and receives one selected-component notification; an unconfirmed number receives only its opt-in, and a STOP suppresses the next publish.
6. Messages fit at most two segments and always retain a permanent update link; component selection follows SUB-004.
7. A mixed email, phone and webhook CSV with components and language dry-runs with created, skipped and refused counts and no writes.
8. Operators add a single email, phone or webhook destination with contact address and selected components through the subscriber form.

Recorded Evidence: `tests/e2e/delivery-contract.test.mjs` (Attributed operator additions, confirmed component scope, private management and opt-out, delivery shard), loading `tests/e2e/helpers/sms-subscriber-flow.mjs` (smsSubscriberFlow) and `tests/e2e/delivery-contract.test.mjs` / `tests/e2e/delivery-contract.test.mjs` (smsBrowserFlow). At the synthetic Twilio HTTP edge, the native public form admits US/Canada E.164 numbers, rejects malformed and disabled-country numbers including non-US/Canada NANP numbers, and admits an owner-enabled country. Repeated opt-ins send one text per number per 24 hours naming the page, frequency, rates and HELP/STOP. Unsigned and wrong-signature replies return 401 without mutations or sends; signed YES records number, actual consent time, page and exact wording once. STOP, UNSUBSCRIBE, CANCEL, END and QUIT each deactivate immediately with one confirmation, including replay deduplication; HELP carries the page and support contact. A selected active number receives one publish, unconfirmed numbers receive only their opt-in, unrelated components send nothing and the next publication after STOP records suppression. Incident, maintenance and report SMS carry intact HTTPS URLs with punctuation outside, literal non-HTTPS schemes, no provider shortening/tracking fields and a complete permanent public link; an overlength update stays within two segments without a partial URL. Mixed email/phone/webhook CSV preview returns created/skipped/refused counts with no writes or sends; operator Add preserves phone components and sends one opt-in. Native browser assertions cover subscribe, Add, country Settings and the SMS composer control with axe, overflow, hydration and full-label checks at 1440/390. `tests/auth-adoption/upgrade.test.mjs` preserves populated 0064 subscriber/token/delivery history through additive `migrations/core/0065_sms_subscriptions.sql`; `tests/auth-adoption/portability.test.mjs` retains the six durable SMS tables; `tests/e2e/delivery-contract.test.mjs` erases SMS consent, replay authority and provider identifiers while preserving another subscriber. `tests/e2e/helpers/subscriber-add-flow.mjs` additionally proves that without provider configuration a valid public phone request returns 503 with no subscriber creation or fallback mail. Optional Twilio environment fields are labelled in setup and declared in seed.json; its nonGoals stay unchanged. Partial proposal, uncounted pending ct judgment.

#### NOTIFY-013 — Redundant email delivery

Status: partial; planning class: HARD; CONTRACT line 3332.

*Tier:* Core · *Cites:* BASE-MAIL-001, BASE-OPS-003 · *Rules:* D-1

**Requirement.** The owner selects primary and secondary providers from SendGrid, Mailgun, Amazon SES, Postmark and Resend, authenticated for the From domain. RL-101 requires safe failover around one durable Lamp notification identity with reconciled provider attempts. The technical profile is specified in [Delivery details](docs/contract/delivery-details.md).

**Acceptance.**
1. Safe explicit retryable non-acceptance lasting five minutes permits secondary submission; an outage before submission also permits switching under NOTIFY-005's identity rule.
2. Ambiguous acceptance stays pending for reconciliation without a second-provider send; a server error alone is never universal proof of non-acceptance.
3. Submitted Message-ID is preserved where supported; SES's assigned identifier is accepted and mapped to the durable Lamp identity.
4. Each provider's documented event authentication is verified honestly, including Postmark Basic authentication rather than a fabricated payload signature.
5. The log records the accepting provider; restoring the primary sends no duplicate.
6. Provider-specific safe-rejection fixtures complete secondary acceptance within seven minutes; each authenticated provider bounce fixture deactivates the address, while uncertain primary submission sends nowhere else.

Recorded Evidence: `tests/e2e/delivery-contract.test.mjs` (NOTIFY-013 NOTIFY-002 NOTIFY-004 NOTIFY-005 NOTIFY-007 NOTIFY-008 NOTIFY-010 — Durable email capture, delivery records and frozen headers), loading `tests/e2e/helpers/mail-failover-flow.mjs`, `tests/e2e/helpers/mail-provider-fixtures.mjs` and `tests/e2e/helpers/mail-settings-browser-flow.mjs` in push Verify’s delivery shard. Real public sign-in, subscription, confirmation, owner Settings, publish, scheduled and Queue doors use only provider network-edge fixtures. Resend 503 failures reach Mailgun, SES or Postmark once at six controlled minutes with a frozen submitted Message-ID, accepting-provider log and no replay after primary recovery; rapid Queue replays do not exhaust the failover deadline. Secondary sending defaults off and requires an explicit owner selection from deployment-connected providers. The production Settings selector and real delivery acceptance row run at 1440/390 with reload persistence, axe, overflow and hydration assertions. Accepted receipts survive disabled or removed secondary configuration, while unconfirmed routing and changed credentials deny a new provider submission. Mailgun HMAC and SES SNS signatures reject invalid fixtures and deactivate hard-bounced addresses; Postmark’s documented Basic authentication is proved without calling it a payload signature. Existing `tests/e2e/rer-mail.test.mjs` and Resend event assertions retain their signed callback coverage. Provider domain reads and disabled tracking are asserted, including explicit disabled SES VDM engagement metrics, no OPEN/CLICK destinations and the configured SNS feedback route. `tests/e2e/rer-mail.test.mjs` pins the stable non-incident Message-ID; populated technical archive journeys retain routing and owner selection.

Recorded Gap: Native provider adapters and controlled failover are evidenced. Reported-outage switching remains unbuilt; safe versus uncertain provider outcomes and reconciliation must meet RL-101 without treating every server error as rejection. Controlled fixtures do not prove mailbox receipt or exact-once mailbox delivery.

Current-main planning reconciliation: Safe/uncertain provider reconciliation exists; outage-before-submission switching is absent. Build provider-specific safe switching, never treat any5xx as proven rejection or capture as mailbox exactly-once.

#### INTAKE-017 — Automation email interpretation

Status: missing; planning class: HARD; CONTRACT line 3359.

*Tier:* Core · *From:* INTAKE-013 · *Cites:* BASE-INPUT-001, BASE-OPS-003

**Requirement.** Automation email follows Statuspage’s documented component semantics, making incompatible capitalization and address suffixes visible.

**Acceptance.**
1. Whole capital DOWN and UP words claim Major outage and release the email claim; where both occur, the first decides.
2. Partial-outage and degraded-performance address suffixes claim those states and never release a claim, so recovery uses the plain address.
3. Pingdom’s documented DOWN alert and UP alert subjects work without extra parsing setup.
4. Lower-case or mixed-case down or up is refused and counted as “keyword not in capitals”.
5. Unknown or rotated tokens, refused senders and subjects with neither keyword are rejected during the mail session and record nothing on a component; refusals are counted with reasons without retaining bodies.
6. Messages are processed in received order and deduplicated by their mail identity.

Recorded Evidence: (none)

Recorded Gap: Lamp has no mail interpretation, mail-session refusal, ordered processing or mail-identity deduplication.

Current-main planning reconciliation: All text interpretation/refusal/order/session-boundary/mail-identity deduplication points remain; depends on INTAKE-013.

**Requirement.** Aggregate traffic counts can alert operators before monitoring notices a problem, without becoming visitor analytics. The counter has no database dependency; configuration is HIST-009 and resolution evidence is HIST-010.

**Acceptance.**
1. Public read requests increment per-minute counters whose stored rows contain only surface, minute and count, with no address, user agent, cookie, query or raw path.
2. The public page, feed and widget still serve and record counters when no database is bound.
3. The baseline uses the previous seven complete UTC same-hour windows; missing hours after collection began count as zero, while absent or zero baselines never infer a spike.
4. A five-times-baseline burst with no unresolved incident creates exactly one stored operator alert and one provider request; the alert names the surface/minute, observed count, baseline mean and app link.
5. Repeated scheduled ticks and consecutive burst minutes create no extra alert or provider request.
6. The same fresh burst during an unresolved incident sends no alert.
7. Traffic alerts create no customer-facing incident or other public content.

#### HIST-010 — Traffic evidence at resolution

Status: partial; planning class: CHEAP; CONTRACT line 3385.

*Tier:* Core · *From:* HIST-006 · *Cites:* BASE-AGENT-006, BASE-AGENT-007, BASE-AGENT-008, BASE-OPS-005

**Requirement.** The AGENT-004 recovery check incorporates current traffic without converting unknown baselines to passing evidence. An agent resolution carries external and live classes; company policy decides the step’s disposition, and any override retains truthful attribution.

**Acceptance.**
1. Only surfaces with a same-hour baseline are traffic evidence; unknown surfaces are listed as learning/not tracked.
2. Elevated traffic refuses unreasoned immediate and backdated resolutions with 409 and preserves the incident and draft.
3. A reasoned immediate override publishes, keeps its reason privately under the operator’s timeline and audit identity, and exposes no reason on the public incident page.
4. An override authorizes only its publication: a later resolution of a reopened incident is checked again.
5. A scheduled resolution is checked again when due; elevated traffic holds its publication and retains its draft.
6. When traffic returns near baseline, that retained schedule publishes without adding an override.

#### HIST-007 — Subscriber reporting

Status: partial; planning class: HARD; CONTRACT line 3386.

#### AUD-002 — Private page access

Status: missing; planning class: HARD; CONTRACT line 3389.

*Tier:* Core · *Cites:* BASE-SIGNIN-001, BASE-ACCESS-002, BASE-ACCESSIBILITY-001 · *Register:* R-80, R-81, R-82

**Requirement.** A private page admits visitors through the access methods its owner selected, while preserving Lamp's failure-tolerant reading and keeping its content out of shared caches. Supersedes `BASE-PUBLIC-001` only for the private-page session cookie; public surfaces retain SUB-005.

**Acceptance.**
1. The owner can choose OIDC through Auth, Okta, Microsoft Entra ID or Google Workspace, or SP-initiated SAML 2.0 with signed assertions, including AD FS, optionally restricted to identity-provider groups.
2. An owner can instead admit an allow-listed email address or email domain through a single-use emailed link, or an IPv4 or IPv6 address within an allowed CIDR range.
3. A visitor each selected access method allows reads the page; a visitor it excludes sees only the page name and sign-in screen, with no incident text.
4. An unsigned or wrongly signed SAML assertion is refused.
5. An admitted visitor still reads the last private snapshot while the database is unavailable.
6. The private-page session has HttpOnly, Secure and SameSite=Lax protections, and no private response is stored in a shared cache.

Recorded Evidence: (none)

Recorded Gap: Private-page access, its configured access methods and retained reading without the database are not built.

Current-main planning reconciliation: All six private visitor OIDC/SAML/email/domain/CIDR admission, signature refusal, private outage reading and cookie/cache points unbuilt; optional operator SSO is not this.

#### API-001 — Token-authenticated management API

Status: partial; planning class: HARD; CONTRACT line 3393.

*Tier:* Core · *Cites:* BASE-ROLE-001, BASE-AUDIT-001, BASE-AGENT-005, BASE-AGENT-006, BASE-AGENT-007

**Requirement.** A scoped management API exposes pages, components, groups, incident drafts and publications, maintenance, templates, subscriber creation/import/deletion, audiences, metrics and automation rules. Supersedes `BASE-ACCESS-002` only for token-authenticated management requests. The management API is documented in [API details](docs/contract/operations-details.md).

**Acceptance.**
1. Owners and Admins can issue tokens scoped to reads, incident writes, component writes, maintenance writes, subscriber writes, metric writes or administration.
2. A token is shown once, held only as a hash, and has an expiry, last-used time and immediate revoke; a revoked token is refused within ten seconds.
3. A token has its own request limit and returns a retryable rate-limit response when exceeded.
4. A component-write token changes a component's state with the token's identity recorded; that token cannot publish an incident.
5. Each write is attributed to the token and its issuing operator in TEAM-007, including the component change visible in the audit view.
6. Incident publication follows UPDATE-001 validation and UPDATE-007 review. An agent publication carries the external and live classes computed from its recipients and affected service; the company's policy decides review or approval.
7. An OpenAPI 3.1 description validates and a generated client completes an incident lifecycle.

Recorded Evidence: `src/core/features/management-api/`, `tests/e2e/helpers/metrics-flow.mjs` (metricsFlow in the existing notifications registration), `tests/auth-adoption/portability.test.mjs` (native rer archive, ownership-authority shard): scoped bearer next-snapshot push, no-scope refusal, token/issuer audit, hash-only storage, last-used time, immediate revoke and inert restored metadata. The read slice uses the same issuance, hash-only bearer authority, immediate revoke and last-used metadata: GET /api/v1/components returns components and groups to an explicit read token; a no-scope or metrics:write-only token receives 403, and a revoked read token receives 401 without usage changes. `tests/e2e/helpers/metrics-flow.mjs` (metricsFlow, existing INTAKE-014 INTAKE-011 INTAKE-009 registration in `tests/e2e/channels-and-presets.test.mjs`, notifications shard) asserts each public-door result. `migrations/core/0064_management_read_tokens.sql` adds opt-in read metadata; `tests/auth-adoption/upgrade.test.mjs` (ACCESS-001 TEAM-001 TEAM-002 upgrade preserves identities, history and subscriber authority and destroys legacy credentials, ownership-upgrade shard) proves populated 0063 metadata survives without acquiring read scope. `tests/e2e/helpers/metrics-portability-flow.mjs` in the existing native archive registration (ownership-authority shard) retains read metadata and refuses its old bearer after restore. No Settings screen changes. Proposal pending ct judgment. `tests/e2e/helpers/metrics-flow.mjs` (metricsFlow, existing INTAKE-014 INTAKE-011 INTAKE-009 registration in `tests/e2e/channels-and-presets.test.mjs`, notifications shard) additionally asserts GET /api/v1/metrics returns the same sanitized metric definitions as the operator door to an explicit read bearer without a cookie; no-scope and metrics:write-only bearers receive 403; immediate revocation returns 401; refusals leave usage metadata unchanged. Datadog, New Relic, Pingdom and Prometheus query fixtures prove neither plaintext nor sealed provider credentials are returned. The existing read authorization, rate limit and final credential fence are shared; no scope or migration is added. Proposal pending ct judgment.

Recorded Gap: Read is built only for components, groups and metric definitions; the other read resources, components:write, incidents:write, maintenance:write, subscribers:write and admin scopes, OpenAPI 3.1 and a generated incident-lifecycle client remain unbuilt. Component-state token attribution, incident-publication refusal and audit-view acceptance remain unproved.

Current-main planning reconciliation: Native read/metrics/subscriber scopes exist; missing incident/component/maintenance/admin writes, exact token+issuer audit, reviewed policy-aware publication and OpenAPI3.1 generated lifecycle client.

4. Each later incident update replies to that account's previous post, and maintenance posts only when selected.

6. A failing X fixture retries and records failure without delaying the page or email, and retroactive updates and imports post nothing.

Detailed source and assertion audits (source inspection, not passing-test claims):


# Public status, theme, components, vendor and uptime audit

Read-only source audit of current main `4c90e6c8f35775e2a0287ab24ccba87d7a8d8485`. Exact scope/status/acceptance came from `packet/contract-inventory.json`. No builds, servers, dependency installs, test execution, fetches, commits, pushes or secrets were used. Assertions below are **inspected tests**, not passing-test claims. This is the only file written by this audit.

Classification: **CHEAP** is bounded local implementation/proof; **HARD** is a substantial local feature or integration; **HELD-EXTERNAL** means actual account/device/provider proof that cannot be substituted locally. A missing feature is not held merely because its eventual hosted proof needs an account. Existing branch work is called out separately and must not be duplicated.

## Immediate queue recommendation

One clean local candidate remains after branch checks: **PAGE-001 acceptance 2 and 3, plus only their PAGE-002 acceptance 1/2 initial-HTML and script-disabled slice**. Build a controlled, populated native fixture with multiple unresolved incidents, more than five published updates per incident, a resolved/draft/private negative control, multiple in-progress maintenance windows and scheduled windows exactly at/beyond the 14-day boundary. Reconcile each incident's exact latest-five bodies/instants/newest-first order, complete permalink timeline, and the complete maintenance membership set in the same response; repeat those customer facts with scripting disabled. Existing source already implements these rules, so this is CHEAP proof work unless that fixture exposes a defect. Do not broaden it into catalogue/order/notes/severity, uptime, generic no-script subscription, or all time-zone proof.

Do **not** queue new THEME-004/005 implementation: real bugs remain on main, but `origin/cloud/sd-lamp-c-theme45` already contains the coherent fix and journey in its ancestry. Adopt/rebase/review that work. Do not queue native catalogue/severity/note work already in the `origin/cloud/sd-lamp-c-f12*` family.

## Material stale inventory findings

- DOCS-001's last claimed hole is closed in inspected current tests: `tests/e2e/start-runtime.test.mjs:105-159` uses a no-script unauthenticated browser, reconciles every index section/title/summary/destination and sidebar, follows every selected page, compares all headings/paragraphs against repository Markdown, and asserts no session cookies. Status-host exclusion is asserted at 431-434.
- THEME-001/002/003 are implemented, despite partial/missing statuses. Settings includes palette/fonts/header art/links/layout/history/CSS/HTML (`src/core/features/page-settings/ui/theme-settings.tsx:29-82`); validators and retained stylesheet/markup integration exist; extensive theme browser and full-capacity journeys are wired by `tests/e2e/publishing-browser.test.mjs:940-942`.
- DEP-001/002 are implemented, despite “Not built.” A twenty-provider catalog, generic formats, guarded scheduled reads, source-attributed history, show/draft/rollup modes, evidence on recovery and release fixture validation exist. DEP-003 operator-alert delivery is still absent in the inspected dependency service/repository.
- PAGE-008's two stated holes have explicit newer assertions: `tests/e2e/snapshot-retained.test.mjs:134-181` proves zero DB touches and reduced immutable artifact-byte reads while every warm request still checks current pointer/freshness.
- The packet's `tests/e2e/helpers/public-freshness-flow.mjs` citation does not exist in current main. Do not rely on it.
- COMP-001 adjacent severity comparisons, COMP-007 ten-second deletion and retained maintenance name, and COMP-010 history/uptime/widget/rollup holes have newer direct assertions. PAGE-002 no-script subscription and PAGE-009 actual relative-clock refresh also now have direct assertions.

## Per-clause findings: documentation and public page

### DOCS-001 — inventory partial; built/proof present; CHEAP metadata reconciliation

`src/routes/docs.index.tsx:1-6` routes the public index; the current complete proof is described above. The old “index usable guidance/navigation unasserted” gap is stale. No new local implementation is justified by that gap. Field proof: none required for this local outcome.

### PAGE-001 — inventory partial; built, remaining bounded completeness proof CHEAP

`public-snapshot/repository.ts:123-179` selects all non-internal components, ordered groups/components, current claims and updates newest-first; `renderer.ts:64-101` derives states/uptime and published incident models; `renderer.ts:142-143` renders exact component note; `renderer.ts:188-198` includes every unresolved incident, five updates, all in-progress maintenance and starts <= generation +14 days. Alternate templates share `templates/parts.ts:153-158` and `templates/history.ts:10-30` (five updates plus complete permanent link).

New current proof already reconciles **native API** component membership across cursor pages (`public-contract.test.mjs:380-399`), exact note escape/boundary (332-337), and representative initial-HTML figure (`public-contract.test.mjs:105-117`). That is stronger than the old packet, but does not reconcile every component's state/note/uptime/daily marks in the single HTML response. The clean new candidate is specifically acceptance 2/3 and matching no-script slice, described above; component point1/4 proof should be reconciled with existing catalogue branches before any additional queue.

### PAGE-002 — inventory partial; significant stale holes; remaining CHEAP proof

Initial HTML and plain POST form are produced server-side (`templates/parts.ts:39-44`, `renderer.ts:132-143`). Current `public-contract.test.mjs:890-935` disables scripts, asserts absolute configured-zone generation instant, zero public polls, no automatic navigation, unchanged document over a new publication/two refresh intervals, and submits the subscription form successfully enough to observe a confirmation email. Thus the old “no-script submit unasserted” gap is stale. Generation exact instant/zone in initial HTML and scripted tooltip are asserted at 635-674.

Remaining: reconcile all PAGE-001 incident/maintenance facts in initial HTML/no-script browser (bounded candidate above), and separately cover every operator entry/timeline/preview time-zone path. `maintenance-timezone.test.mjs:58-164` already exercises configured-zone scheduling/rescheduling and incident opened-at entry; do not claim all zones/paths absent. No live external account required.

### PAGE-005 — inventory partial; read-path/recovery holes largely stale; CHEAP trigger matrix proof

The service builds a complete artifact set before pointer advancement (`public-snapshot/service.ts:185-273`), retries pending work (384-415), reads retained artifacts without repository access (322-368), and renderer builds page/feed/widget together (`renderer.ts:248-276`). `snapshot-retained.test.mjs:134-181` directly asserts zero database access and immutable caching. At 182-206, an always-failing replica publication plus object-storage outage still reads the prior known outage, including cold restart. `snapshot-release-rebuild-flow.mjs` is wired at `public-contract.test.mjs:26` and covers release rebuild/retry/reset generation. Remaining exact acceptance1 proof is the complete PAGE-001 change-trigger matrix; do not rebuild the already implemented mechanism. No external blocker.

### PAGE-006 — inventory partial; most listed outage holes now covered; CHEAP boundary proof

`snapshot-retained.test.mjs:55-64` saves page/Atom/widget/badge bytes, injects queue failure and reads them unchanged. A throwing repository with real public routes/R2/durable replica is set up at 67-69. At 135-206, page/Atom/widget/JSON/badges retain known state through database access refusal, object-storage failure, failed replica writes and cold restart; stale headers/body and generation instant are asserted. # Delivery, subscription, maintenance, integration and mobile audit

Read-only audit of the supplied `run-lamp` checkout at HEAD `4c90e6c8f35775e2a0287ab24ccba87d7a8d8485` (17 November 2026 audit). Only this packet document was written. No product changes, test execution, build, launch, install, fetch, push, commit, provider access or secret reading occurred. Source and assertions below were inspected; their presence does not assert that a test passes. Inventory statuses are retained as historical judgments, not promoted by this audit.

Classifications: **CHEAP** means a bounded implementation or missing assertion using existing native records/doors; **HARD** means multiple lifecycle/storage/transport systems or significant integration work; **HELD-EXTERNAL** means a real provider/device/hosted outcome remains outside source-only proof. A clause can have both a local implementation remainder and a separate external proof obligation.

## Main findings and independent builder choices

Four strong independent work packages remain, with a fifth small maintenance improvement and one optional social-log package. These are product outcomes, not invitations to rewrite already-built features. Do not create a new NOTIFY-003 builder: an existing unmerged branch owns that work.

| Priority | Bounded package and buyer value | Exact deliverable and proof | Classification / review gate |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **SUB-018 public verified webhook creation and format selection**. A customer can onboard without asking the operator. | Add a general script-free public webhook form with endpoint, contact, components/preferences and Lamp/Statuspage choice. Pending until contact verification; no destination event before confirmation; retain neutral duplicate responses, daily confirmation limits, public rate/cross-site checks, safe endpoint checks, capacity and prelaunch refusal. Explicitly preserve private/CSV/token-management formats. Also add format choice to existing incident-follow webhook form or document that door's explicit format handling. Exercise new/existing/unsafe/cross-site/full/prelaunch requests, pending silence, confirmation, readback, selected receiver and both formats at 1440/390. | **CHEAP-to-HARD bounded** (existing consent/rendering exist; public abuse and SSRF boundaries are the risk). Review **authorization/data** edges; no reason to gate ordinary local work or wait for a real receiver. |

Assert no tracking pixel/tracked link on captured nonempty messages. Keep social log outside this package unless separately scoped. | **CHEAP**. ## Separate field-proof lane

These are neither source-edit completion claims nor ordinary builder permission blockers. Prepare reproducible local fixtures/artifacts first, then the owner-authorized runner can collect installation-scoped evidence:

1. **Mail**: captured raw delivered message with cryptographic DKIM verification covering both List-Unsubscribe headers, reachable sender DKIM/DMARC setup/deploy checks and actual receipt/bounce/complaint outcomes for selected providers. Controlled authenticated callbacks establish application behavior, not actual inbox receipt.
2. **SMS**: Twilio test-account consent/YES/STOP/three-delivered-receipt/literal-link journey; any Plivo failover/signature claim needs its own provider evidence. Keep credentials out of packet/report.
3. **Calendar**: Google Calendar, Outlook and Apple Calendar add/reschedule/cancel using same stable UID, with managed/native email .ics attachment receipt. Local RFC5545 parser checks are necessary but do not prove calendar-client behavior.
4. **Scale**: hosted Cloudflare Queues/D1 seeded run with outside fake successful receivers, exact commit/install identity, admitted counts, no duplicate or missing identity, fixed recipient capture, overlap/order and channel completion timestamps. No release-limit raise based on a predicted rate.
5. **Chat/social/push/support desks**: real Slack/Teams destination confirmation, X owner application/tier, Bluesky/Mastodon account receipts; installed push/device revocation across required platforms; support sidebar test installation. Controlled edges are valuable local integration proof with narrower claim.

Only gate a builder for a real unresolved authorization, data, money or migration boundary. Routine reversible code/proof work should proceed. An external receipt is a separate held proof task, not justification for rebuilding code already present or promoting a source-only assertion to field acceptance.

Terminal main refresh: origin/main advanced from 4c90e6c8f35775e2a0287ab24ccba87d7a8d8485 to c77030f1bf9501975efca38be82a33d9611a9d5a during planning. The single newly landed health-ledger commit changes only deploy/setup/import ledger tooling and its tests/docs, not CONTRACT.md, ACCEPTANCE.md, docs/CLAIMS.md or the audited product feature implementations. Read-only inspection of that delta preserves these findings; no authored product changes occurred. The former health-ledger candidate is now main, not in flight.


# Incident, update, intake, brief, history, specialist and agent-surface audit

Read-only source audit of `just-every/run-lamp` at `4c90e6c8f35775e2a0287ab24ccba87d7a8d8485`, using the 58 open rows in `packet/contract-inventory.json`. Paths below are relative to `run-lamp/`; line numbers refer to that checkout. Source and test bodies were inspected. **No tests, build, launch, dependency installation, fetch, commit or push were performed.** An assertion present in a test is evidence of an implemented proof, not a claim that this audit executed it or that hosted acceptance passed.

Classification: **CHEAP** = bounded local implementation/proof or stale evidence repair; **HARD** = substantial missing product capability, new integration/authority surface or several dependent capabilities; **HELD-EXTERNAL** = actual company-policy/provider/field acceptance unavailable outside Lamp. Mixed rows separate these portions. A missing Base company-policy decision API does not block local domain code, deterministic class computation, registry construction or local conformance fixtures. It does block claiming real company-policy decisions, review-feed waits and all-autonomous/held-policy parity.

## Main findings

* The inventory is stale in several places. Complete newer source-level proof already exists for immutable corrections/deletion, all impact choices and uptime preservation, authenticated intake boundaries, public Markdown/link literalness, Teams literal schemes/HTML, raw Lamp webhook fields, report timing/channel choice, cited report refusal/immutability, brief no-model/disclosure parity, and serial traffic settings. Do not create duplicate builders merely to implement these again.
* **UPDATE-007 has a real, bounded data-loss gap:** reviewer and review time exist only on `incident_drafts` (`migrations/core/0004_publishing_contract.sql:8`; `src/core/features/incidents/repository.ts:204`). Publication inserts no reviewer/time into the immutable update (`publication-repository.ts:32`), deletes the reviewed draft (`:74`), and its audit payload contains no review metadata (`:75`). The published record therefore loses the approval provenance promised by acceptance 2. Preserve the exact review revision, reviewer and review instant in the publication transaction and prove readback after the draft is consumed. This is a CHEAP implementation gap on main, but an existing sd branch already implements it; exclude a duplicate new builder (see prior-work appendix).
* **UPDATE-005 has a contract/source tension:** `draft.ts:19` deliberately carries the last published affected-audience wording into the next draft, and `tests/e2e/helpers/published-incident-state.mjs:22` expressly asserts that behavior. Acceptance says an empty next-update draft. Impact, still-working and details are empty, but audience is not. Do not silently declare the complete empty-draft obligation passed; reconcile this exact requirement while preserving evidence history.
* Real larger gaps remain in audience variants, Oncall, inbound automation email, continuous story comparison, early warning, translation, outside-agent MCP access, maintenance drafting, and the MCP registry/computed classes/event/discovery/week journeys. These cannot be erased by relabeling existing HTTP endpoints or extension events.
* AUTO-001 owner-approved automation is substantially implemented. Its remaining agent class/policy work should not be confused with absence of owner rules. AUTO-003's declared missing vendor-staleness, mirror-lag and SMS-cap carriers remain real; even SMS sending exists now, but a spend-cap carrier is not present.
